## Related Work

LLM-based multi-agent systems. LLM-based multi-<sub>agen</sub>t <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>ems organ</sub>i<sub>ze mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e</sub> l<sub>anguage-mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>a</sub>b<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>wor</sub>kfl<sub>ows,</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>exc</sub>h<sub>ange</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erme</sub>di<sub>-</sub> <sub>a</sub>t<sub>e so</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons, cr</sub>iti<sub>que one ano</sub>th<sub>er, an</sub>d <sub>aggrega</sub>t<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>-</sub> sions (Du et al. 2024; Wu et al. 2024; Li et al. 2023; Qian et al. 2024; Hon<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024). The<sub>y</sub> build on decom<sub>p</sub>osed <sub>reason</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> <sub>se</sub>lf<sub>-cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>-perspec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> d<sub>e</sub>lib<sub>era-</sub> tion to im<sub>p</sub>rove LLM reliabilit<sub>y</sub> (Wei et al. 2022; Wan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2023; Kojima et al. 2022), and have been summarized in recent surve<sub>y</sub>s on a<sub>g</sub>ent workflows and a<sub>pp</sub>lications (Guo et al. 2024; Li et al. 2024; Chen et al. 2024). Our work focuses <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an new ro</sub>l<sub>es or</sub> p<sup>rom</sup>p<sup>tin</sup>g <sup>strate</sup>g<sup>ies</sup>.

Communication topology optimization. Task-level causal contribution. For each active edge $e \in$ E of the generated graph $G = ( V , E )$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n-</sub> tervene<sup>d</sup> <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub><sup>h</sup> $G ^ { - { \check { e } } } = G \backslash \{ e \}$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>re-execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> LLM<sub>-</sub>MAS<sub>.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t $Q ( x , G )$ <sub>an</sub>d $\bar { Q } ( x , G ^ { - e } )$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k scores obtained before and after intervening on e, respectivel . We define the task-level causal contribution of e as

$$
\Delta _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { t a s k } } = Q ( x , G ) - Q ( x , G ^ { - e } ) .\tag{3}
$$

A positive value indicates that retaining edge e improves th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>come re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>o remov</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> it<sub>, w</sub>h<sub>ereas a nega-</sub> ti<sub>ve va</sub>l<sub>ue sugges</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>ransm</sub>itt<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>roug</sub>h e may be detrimental to the task outcome. Since our expla-<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>ocuses on commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>suppor</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>a</sub>b<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on, we re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e nonnega</sub>ti<sub>ve preserva</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub>

$$
u _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { t a s k } } = \mathrm { m a x } \left( 0 , \Delta _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { t a s k } } \right) .\tag{4}
$$

A larger value provides stronger evidence that edge e cont<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>es</sub> t<sub>o preserv</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>or o</sub>f th<sub>e can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> <sub>zero</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>no</sub> <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>preserva</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c.</sub>

Auxiliary semantic signal. The final task outcome can be t<sub>oo coarse</sub> t<sub>o</sub> di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ngu</sub>i<sub>s</sub>h <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge mas</sub>k<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>rece</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>score.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>use</sub> <sub>resu</sub>lt<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> <sub>en</sub>t<sub>ropy</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>an aux</sub>ili<sub>ary s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l t<sub>o c</sub>h<sub>arac</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ze</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l res<sub>p</sub>onse (Kuhn, Gal, and Farquhar 2023; Farquhar et al. 2024). For the response set Y obtained from M stochastic <sub>execu</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>group</sub> <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>equ</sub>i<sub>va</sub>l<sub>en</sub>t <sub>responses</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> classes C(Y) and compute

$$
\bar { H } ( \mathcal { Y } ) = - \sum _ { c \in \mathcal { C } ( \mathcal { Y } ) } p ( c ) \log p ( c ) ,\tag{5}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $p ( c )$ is the empirical frequency of class c among the M executions. We use $M = 5$ i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ex-</sub> <sub>ecu</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f<sub>or</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>grap</sub>h <sub>an</sub>d <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge-mas</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d graph. The auxiliary semantic efect of edge e is defined as

u _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { s e m } } = \operatorname* { m a x } \left( 0 , \bar { H } ( \mathcal { Y } ^ { G ^ { - e } } ) - \bar { H } ( \mathcal { Y } ^ { G } ) \right) .\tag{6}
$$

A positive value indicates that masking e increases the un-<sub>cer</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>y o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>response, prov</sub>idi<sub>ng a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e e</sub>d<sub>ge con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>es</sub> t<sub>o preserv</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>or o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>.</sub>

$$

w<sup>h</sup>ere e<sup>·</sup> <sup>d</sup>eno<sup>t</sup>es norma<sup>li</sup>za<sup>ti</sup>on w<sup>ithi</sup>n <sup>th</sup>e curren<sup>t</sup> grap<sup>h</sup> or <sub>ca</sub>lib<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>, an</sub>d $\alpha , \beta \geq 0$ <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>s.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub>

Budget-Aware Subgraph Selection. The budget $\begin{array} { r l } { b } & { { } = } \end{array}$ $( \rho _ { E } , \rho _ { V } )$ <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d <sub>re</sub>t<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>os</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>no</sub>d<sub>es.</sub> F<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>,</sub> th<sub>ese ra</sub>ti<sub>os are conver</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> <sub>re</sub>t<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $k _ { E } ( b )$ <sub>an</sub>d $k _ { V } ( b )$ <sub>accor</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>grap</sub>h <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ze.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t th<sub>e mos</sub>t i<sub>mpor</sub>t<sub>an</sub>t <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges accor</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e preserva</sub>ti<sub>on or</sub>d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d f<sub>rom e</sub>d<sub>ge a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub>

$$
E _ { b } ^ { \star } = \mathrm { T o p K } \left( \pi _ { G } , k _ { E } ( b ) \right) .\tag{9}
$$

The node budget is subsequently enforced jointly with the structural projection described below to obtain an executable <sub>commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on su</sub>b<sub>grap</sub>h<sub>.</sub>

Executability-Constrained Projection. To transform the <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>-u</sub>tilit<sub>y e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o an execu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> topology, we apply an executability-constrained projection:

$$
H _ { b } ^ { \star } = \mathcal { R } _ { \mathrm { v a l i d } } ( G , E _ { b } ^ { \star } , k _ { V } ( b ) ) .\tag{10}
$$

The projection $\mathcal { R } _ { \mathrm { v a l i d } }$ <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l edge set E and never introduces new links. Therefore, when the original topolog<sub>y</sub> G is a directed ac<sub>y</sub>clic graph, the ext<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>su</sub>b<sub>grap</sub>h <sub>na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>preserves</sub> <sub>acyc</sub>li<sub>c</sub>it<sub>y.</sub> S<sub>pec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> the projection retains mandatory input and final-aggregation <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s, removes commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> li<sub>n</sub>k<sub>s</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>id<sub>en</sub>t t<sub>o exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s, an</sub>d di<sub>scar</sub>d<sub>s op</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>no</sub> l<sub>onger</sub> <sub>par</sub>ti<sub>c</sub>i<sub>pa</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>va</sub>lid di<sub>rec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>pa</sub>th<sub>s.</sub> Wh<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>no</sub>d<sub>e</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>ve,</sub> <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>or</sub>iti<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>accor</sub>d<sub>-</sub> i<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e aggrega</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>preserva</sub>ti<sub>on u</sub>tiliti<sub>es o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>id<sub>en</sub>t <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> $H _ { b } ^ { \star }$ th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore sa</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>fi<sub>es</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e</sub> <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d th<sub>e execu</sub>ti<sub>on requ</sub>i<sub>remen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e u</sub>nd<sub>e</sub>rl<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> LLM<sub>-</sub>MAS<sub>.</sub>

Budget-Specific Explanation Family. Applying Eq. (10) to the bud<sub>g</sub>et set B <sub>y</sub>ields an ex<sub>p</sub>lanation famil<sub>y</sub>

$$
{ \mathcal { F } } ^ { \star } ( x , G ) = \{ H _ { b } ^ { \star } : b \in B \} .\tag{11}
$$

For each query $x _ { i }$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e grap</sub>h $\bar { G _ { i } }$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e ex</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on proce</sub>d<sub>ure pro</sub>d<sub>uces one</sub> <sup>tar</sup>g<sup>et</sup> <sup>s</sup>u<sup>b</sup>g<sup>ra</sup>p<sup>h</sup> $H _ { i , b } ^ { \star }$ f<sub>or</sub> <sub>every</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t $b \in B$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>ese</sub> t<sub>a</sub>r<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s</sub> f<sub>orm</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng se</sub>t

$$

Explainer mapping. We train a parameterized explainer $F _ { \theta }$ to a<sub>pp</sub>rox<sup>i</sup>mate t<sup>h</sup>e <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub><sup>h</sup>-to-su<sup>b</sup><sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub><sup>h</sup> ma<sub>pp</sub><sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub> <sup>i</sup>n<sup>d</sup>uce<sup>d</sup> b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e causa</sub>l <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on proce</sub>d<sub>ure:</sub>

$$

Training objective. Let $A _ { i , b , \epsilon } ^ { \star }$ indicate whether edge e be-<sup>l</sup>on<sub>g</sub>s to $H _ { i , b } ^ { \star } ,$ <sub>, an</sub>d l<sub>e</sub>t $R _ { i , b , v } ^ { \star }$ indicate whether node v is ret<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e exp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ner pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e correspon</sub>di<sub>ng pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bil<sub>-</sub> iti<sub>es</sub> $\widehat { A } _ { i , b , e }$ <sub>an</sub>d $\widehat { R } _ { i , b , v }$ <sub>, w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>are co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e e</sub>d<sub>ge-</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>no</sub>d<sub>e-re</sub>t<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on vec</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> $\widehat { \mathbf { A } } _ { i , b }$ <sub>an</sub>d $\widehat { \mathbf { R } } _ { i , b } .$ <sub>,</sub> res<sub>p</sub>ecti<sub>v</sub>el<sub>y.</sub> We <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { e d g e } } = \sum _ { ( x _ { i } , G _ { i } , b , H _ { i , b } ^ { \star } ) \in \mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { e x p } } } \frac { 1 } { | E _ { i } | } \sum _ { e \in E _ { i } } \mathrm { B C E } \left( A _ { i , b , e } ^ { \star } , \widehat { A } _ { i , b , e } \right) ,
$$

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { n o d e } } = \sum _ { ( x _ { i } , G _ { i } , b , H _ { i , b } ^ { \star } ) \in \mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { e x p } } } \frac { 1 } { \vert V _ { i } \vert } \sum _ { v \in V _ { i } } \mathrm { B C E } \left( R _ { i , b , v } ^ { \star } , \widehat { R } _ { i , b , v } \right) ,\tag{14}
$$

with the final objective

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathcal { L } = \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { e d g e } } + \lambda _ { \mathrm { n o d e } } \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { n o d e } } + \lambda _ { \mathrm { w d } } \Vert \theta \Vert _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } . } \end{array}\tag{15}
$$

Subgraph prediction. The predicted probabilities are first <sub>conver</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>-compa</sub>tibl<sub>e can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e se</sub>t<sub>s:</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \widehat { E } _ { i , b } = \mathrm { T o p K } \left( \widehat { \mathbf { A } } _ { i , b } , k _ { E } ( b ) \right) , } \\ & { \widehat { V } _ { i , b } = \mathrm { T o p K } \left( \widehat { \mathbf { R } } _ { i , b } , k _ { V } ( b ) \right) . } \end{array}\tag{16}
$$

$$
\widehat { H } _ { i , b } = F _ { \boldsymbol { \theta } } ( \boldsymbol { x } _ { i } , G _ { i } , b ) = { \mathcal { R } } _ { \mathrm { v a l i d } } \left( G _ { i } , \widehat { E } _ { i , b } , \widehat { V } _ { i , b } \right) ,\tag{17}
$$

## Experiment

## Experimental Setup

Datasets and backbone. We evaluate on six benchmarks: AQuA, GSM8K, MultiArith, and SVAMP for mathematical reasonin<sub>g</sub> (Lin<sub>g</sub> et al. 2017; Cobbe et al. 2021; Ro<sub>y</sub> and Roth 2015; Patel, Bhattamishra, and Go<sub>y</sub>al 2021), MMLU for knowled<sub>g</sub>e-intensive reasonin<sub>g</sub> (Hendr<sub>y</sub>cks et al. 2021), and HumanEval for code <sub>g</sub>eneration (Chen et al. 2021). All methods use Qwen3-8B as the backbone LLM (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2025), with Vanilla, CoT (Wei et al. 2022), and SC(CoT) (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2023) as sin<sub>g</sub>le-a<sub>g</sub>ent baselines.

Candidate generators and calibration. $$

where ∥ denotes concatenation and $\mathbf { g } _ { G }$ i<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>permu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub> i<sub>nvar</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>summary.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>prun</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>os</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>en-</sub> <sub>co</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub>

$$

## Graph-to-Subgraph Supervision and Decoding

F<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>ca</sub>lib<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>on pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> $( x _ { i } , G _ { i } )$ <sub>, causa</sub>l <sub>su</sub>b<sub>grap</sub>h <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc-</sub> t<sup>i</sup>on <sub>p</sub>ro<sup>d</sup>uces one tar<sub>g</sub>et $H _ { i , b } ^ { \star }$ f<sub>or every</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t $b \in B$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>se</sub>t i<sub>s</sub>

E<sub>ac</sub>h t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>su</sub>b<sub>grap</sub>h d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>nes</sub> bi<sub>nary</sub> <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge-</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>no</sub>d<sub>e-re</sub>t<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub>

At i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> ti<sub>me,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>biliti<sub>es</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>conver</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e se</sub>t<sub>s accor</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e grap</sub>h<sub>-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c re</sub>t<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> counts:

## Causal Supervision Construction Details

Repeated execution and task score. For every active edge $e ,$ we independentl<sub>y</sub> execute both the original graph G and th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ervene</sub>d <sub>grap</sub>h $G ^ { - e }$ f<sub>or</sub> $M = 5$ <sub>runs.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>score</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e mean ou</sub>t<sub>come over</sub> th<sub>ese execu</sub>ti<sub>ons:</sub>

$$
Q ( x , G ) = \frac { 1 } { M } \sum _ { m = 1 } ^ { M } Q ^ { ( m ) } ( x , G ) .\tag{27}
$$

Th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>grap</sub>h i<sub>s re-execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge</sub> <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>one</sub> <sub>response</sub> <sub>se</sub>t <sub>across</sub> <sub>a</sub>ll i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erven</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> All executions use a decoding temperature of 0.2. The origi-<sub>na</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ervene</sub>d <sub>runs</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> <sub>an</sub>d d<sub>o</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>use pa</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om see</sub>d<sub>s.</sub> Th<sub>e same response se</sub>t<sub>s are use</sub>d t<sub>o compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e mean</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>come an</sub>d th<sub>e aux</sub>ili<sub>ary</sub> <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>c s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>.</sub>

Result-level semantic signal. Semantic entropy is com-<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>em</sub> <sub>responses,</sub> <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>oca</sub>l <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>id<sub>en</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> <sub>an</sub> <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ce</sub> <sub>a</sub>li<sub>gns</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>aux</sub>ili<sub>ary</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>em-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>quan-</sub> tity whose causal contribution is being estimated. The M fi-<sub>na</sub>l <sub>responses</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>groupe</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> <sub>equ</sub>i<sub>va</sub>l<sub>ence</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>asses</sub> <sub>us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e same equ</sub>i<sub>va</sub>l<sub>ence proce</sub>d<sub>ure</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ervene</sub>d <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>s,</sub> <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er</sub> <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h th<sub>e</sub> <sub>emp</sub>i<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub> <sub>en</sub>t<sub>ropy</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> <sub>paper.</sub>

Score normalization. The mean task outcome and its nonnegative intervention diference already lie in [0, 1]. We th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on on</sub> it<sub>s or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub> th<sub>e seman</sub>ti<sub>c con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e max</sub>i<sub>mum</sub> empirical entropy of M samples:

$$
\widetilde { u } _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { t a s k } } = u _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { t a s k } } , \qquad \widetilde { u } _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { s e m } } = \operatorname* { m i n } \left( 1 , \frac { u _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { s e m } } } { \log M } \right) .\tag{28}
$$

Th<sub>e com</sub>bi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>preserva</sub>ti<sub>on u</sub>tilit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>en compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith $( \alpha , \beta ) = ( 0 . 8 , \bar { 0 . 2 } )$ .

Pruning counts and validity projection. The budget $b =$ $( \rho _ { E } , \rho _ { V } )$ ) specifies removal ratios. For a candidate graph $G =$ $( V , E )$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e num</sub>b<sub>ers o</sub>f <sub>remove</sub>d <sub>e</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>s are</sub>

$$
r _ { E } ( b ) = \lceil \rho _ { E } | E | \rceil , \qquad r _ { V } ( b ) = \lceil \rho _ { V } | V | \rceil ,\tag{29}
$$

<sub>w</sub>ith <sub>re</sub>t<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on coun</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $k _ { E } ( b ) = | E | - r _ { E } ( b )$ <sub>an</sub>d $k _ { V } ( b ) =$ $| V | - r _ { V } ( b )$ . Thus, E25 denotes removing 25% of the active edges and N20 denotes removing 20% of the active nodes. Th<sub>e remove</sub>d M<sub>a</sub>th<sub>ema</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l A<sub>na</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>t i<sub>s correc</sub>t<sub>,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t it<sub>s</sub> d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>up</sub>li<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on a</sub>l<sub>rea</sub>d<sub>y ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>th<sub>er ro</sub>l<sub>es an</sub>d i<sub>s</sub> f<sub>orwar</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> d<sub>owns</sub>t<sub>ream agen</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> R<sub>e-</sub> <sub>mov</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> thi<sub>s no</sub>d<sub>e s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t<sub>ens</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f <sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>s an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> <sub>promp</sub>t<sub>s rece</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>d d<sub>owns</sub>t<sub>ream.</sub> T<sub>o</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l <sub>on</sub>li<sub>ne usage</sub> f<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>s</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> 6,383 to 4,567 tokens, a reduction of28.5%, while the answer <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>ns correc</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Case 2: Wrong → Wrong under a shared domain misconception. ## Algorithmic Details

## Causal Supervision Construction and Explainer Prediction

Al<sub>gor</sub>ith<sub>ms</sub> 1 <sub>an</sub>d 2 <sub>summar</sub>i<sub>ze</sub> th<sub>e o</sub>fli<sub>ne cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>-</sub>t<sub>o-su</sub>b<sub>grap</sub>h <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>an</sub>d t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>-</sub>ti<sub>me</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on proce</sub>d<sub>ures, respec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y.</sub>

Al<sub>gor</sub>ith<sub>m</sub> 1<sub>:</sub> C<sub>ausa</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> G<sub>roun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d S<sub>uperv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> C<sub>ons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub>   
Require: $\mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { c a l } } , \mathcal { G } _ { \phi } , \boldsymbol { B } , M , \alpha , \beta$   
Ensure: Graph-to-subgraph dataset $\mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { e x p l } }$   
1<sub>:</sub> $\mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { e x p l } }  \mathit { \emptyset }$   
2: for each $x \in \mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { c a l } }$ do   
3<sub>:</sub> $G = ( V , E )  { } \mathcal { G } _ { \phi } ( x )$   
4<sub>:</sub> for each active $\mathsf { e d g e }  { e } \in E$ do   
5<sub>:</sub> $( Q _ { G } ^ { e } , \mathcal { V } ^ { G , e } ) \mathrel { \mathop {  } } \mathrm { E v a L U A T E } ( x , G , M )$   
6<sub>:</sub> $\smash { \dot { G } ^ { - e } \gets \mathrm { I N T E R V E N E } ( G , e ) }$   
7<sub>:</sub> $( Q _ { e } , y ^ { G ^ { - e } } ) \gets \mathrm { E v a L U A T E } ( x , G ^ { - e } , M )$   
8<sub>:</sub> $u _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { t a s k } } \gets \operatorname* { m a x } ( 0 , Q _ { G } ^ { e } - Q _ { e } )$   
9: $u _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { s e m } } \gets \operatorname* { m a x } \Big ( 0 , \bar { H } ( \mathcal { Y } ^ { G ^ { - e } } ) - \bar { H } ( \mathcal { Y } ^ { G , e } ) \Big )$   
10<sub>:</sub> $\widetilde { u } _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { t a s k } }  u _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { t a s k } }$   
11<sub>:</sub> $\widetilde { u } _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { s e m } } \gets \operatorname* { m i n } ( 1 , u _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { s e m } } / \log M )$   
12<sub>:</sub> $\begin{array} { r } { u _ { e } \gets \mathrm { c l i p } _ { [ 0 , 1 ] } ( \alpha \widetilde { u } _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { t a s k } } + \beta \widetilde { u } _ { e } ^ { \mathrm { s e m } } ) } \end{array}$   
13<sub>:</sub> end for   
14<sub>:</sub> $\pi _ { G }  \operatorname { S }$ <sub>ort</sub>D<sub>escending</sub> $( E , \{ u _ { e } \} _ { e \in E } )$   
15<sub>:</sub> for each budget $b \in B \cdot$ do   
16<sub>:</sub> $( k _ { E } , k _ { V } )  1$ RetentionCounts(G, b)   
17<sub>:</sub> $E _ { b } ^ { \star } \gets \mathrm { T o r K } ( \pi _ { G } , k _ { E } )$   
18<sub>:</sub> $H _ { b } ^ { \star } \gets \mathcal { R } _ { \mathrm { v a l i d } } ( G , E _ { b } ^ { \star } , k _ { V } )$   
19<sub>:</sub> $\mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { e x p l } }  \mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { e x p l } } \cup \{ ( x , G , b , H _ { b } ^ { \star } ) \}$   
20<sub>:</sub> end for   
21: end for   
22: return $\mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { e x p l } }$

Al<sub>gor</sub>ith<sub>m</sub> 2<sub>:</sub> E<sub>xp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ner</sub> T<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d T<sub>es</sub>t<sub>-</sub>Ti<sub>me</sub> P<sub>re</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on</sub>   
Require: $\mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { e x p l } } , \lambda _ { \mathrm { n o d e } } , \lambda _ { \mathrm { w d } }$   
Ensure: Trained explainer $F _ { \theta }$   
1: Initialize parameters θ   
Training   
2: for each minibatch $\mathcal { S } \subseteq \mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { e x p l } }$ do   
3<sub>:</sub> for each $( x _ { i } , G _ { i } , b , H _ { i , b } ^ { \star } ) \dot { = } \mathcal { S }$ do   
4<sub>:</sub> $( { \bf A } _ { i , b } ^ { \star } , { \bf R } _ { i , b } ^ { \star } ) \gets$ I<sub>ndicators</sub> $\left( G _ { i } , H _ { i , b } ^ { \star } \right)$   
5<sub>:</sub> $( \widehat { \mathbf { A } } _ { i , b } , \widehat { \mathbf { R } } _ { i , b } ) \gets F _ { \theta } ( x _ { i } , G _ { i } , b )$   
6<sub>:</sub> end for   
7<sub>:</sub> <sup>C</sup>om<sub>p</sub>ute $\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { e d g e } }$ <sub>an</sub>d $\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { n o d e } }$ on S   
8<sub>:</sub> ${ \mathcal { L } } \gets { \mathcal { L } } _ { \mathrm { e d g e } } + \lambda _ { \mathrm { n o d e } } { \mathcal { L } } _ { \mathrm { n o d e } } + \lambda _ { \mathrm { w d } } \| { \boldsymbol { \theta } } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 }$   
9: U<sub>p</sub>date θ b<sub>y</sub> minimizin<sub>g</sub> L   
10: end for   
Test-time prediction   
11: Receive query x, graph G, and budget b   
12<sub>:</sub> $( \widehat { \mathbf { A } } _ { b } , \widehat { \mathbf { R } } _ { b } ) \gets F _ { \theta } ( x , G , b )$   
13<sub>:</sub> $( k _ { E } , k _ { V } )  \mathrm { ~ F ~ }$ R<sub>etention</sub>C<sub>ounts</sub> $( G , b )$   
14<sub>:</sub> $\widehat { E } _ { b } \gets \mathrm { T o r K } ( \widehat { \mathbf { A } } _ { b } , k _ { E } )$   
15<sub>:</sub> $\widehat { V } _ { b } \gets \mathrm { T o r K } ( \widehat { \mathbf { R } } _ { b } , k _ { V } )$   
16<sub>:</sub> $\widehat { H } _ { b } \gets \mathcal { R } _ { \mathrm { v a l i d } } ( G , \widehat { E } _ { b } , \widehat { V } _ { b } )$   
17: return $\widehat { H } _ { b }$
